When a doc announces that a line is dead, that announcement is a **search string** — grep the sibling for the dead line immediately.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 14) — THESIS-BREAK MODE: staleness stops being a drift class and becomes a totality.** All prior STANDING MONITORS assume incremental drift against a still-live frame. On 2026-08-07 the frame itself retired (leg-1 SPF fired, no successor declared) and BOTH trade docs are 100% pre-print vintage — nearly every load-bearing sentence, not a handful of cells, is now wrong. **The taxonomy still applies (STALE-MARK / STALE-FRAMING / TRIGGER-STATUS-DRIFT / CAL-DRIFT) but volume alone signals a different mode is needed**: don't line-edit a dead frame's watch-for-entry tables cell-by-cell — surface the totality as an ESCALATION asking SAM to choose compress-to-history vs correct-in-place BEFORE running a normal sweep (see NEXT RUN HINTS Run 15). Recognize this mode by: (a) the retiring doc (STATUS/THESIS) explicitly says "no successor frame declared," and (b) a grep for the dead frame's central term (here, `MED-HIGH`/`convexity-tail`) returns >15 current-voice hits across both trade docs.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 14) — A "STANDS DOWN" / "VOID" ruling is a stronger claim than "stale" and needs its own grep.** STATUS didn't just let TRY-FIRE-007 or the −153K/85% line go stale — it explicitly ruled TRY-FIRE-007 STANDS DOWN and −153K/85% VOID (a future re-build "re-arms nothing"). TRADE.md still asserted "TRY-FIRE-007 unaffected" — not stale, actively **contradicted**. Whenever a canonical doc issues a STANDS DOWN / VOID / RETIRED ruling on a specific named object (a card ID, a threshold line, a gate), grep both trade docs for that exact object name — a "still fine" claim on a ruled-void object is a correctness bug, not a freshness one, and should be reported ahead of ordinary STALE-MARK items.

- **🆕 NEW (post Run 15) — ONCE A DOC IS COMPRESSED-TO-HISTORY, THE BANNER IS THE ONLY THING THAT CAN ROT — AND IT ROTS ON BASIS CORRECTIONS.** After the 8/7 compress ruling, TRADE/STRATEGY interiors are frozen audit trail by design and must NOT be re-flagged. But the banner is **current voice**, it **quotes figures**, and it **certifies a DO-NOT-TOUCH list** — three rot surfaces. Both fired within 10 days: the quoted `25.3% of the −180K peak` / `−108K` pair was retired by the 8/11 basis correction (R = −188,077 ⇒ 24.2%, leg-1 line −112,846), and the "still valid, deliberately NOT touched" certification of the Meiji ~4.0% floor was overtaken by the 30Y closing through 4.00%. **Every run on a compressed doc: read ONLY the banner, and check (a) every figure in it against the current basis, (b) every "still valid" certification against what has happened since it was written.** A certification has a *date of measurement* even when it carries no number.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 15) — A STALE `Last Updated:` LINE MANUFACTURES FALSE PREMISES IN THE PEOPLE WHO SPAWN YOU.** TRADE.md reads `Last Updated: 2026-08-02 Sun` while carrying edits from **8/7 and 8/10**; STRATEGY.md reads the same while carrying its 8/7 apply. **This exact drift caused the Run-15 spawn brief to instruct METSUKE that both docs were probably un-applied 8/2-vintage** — a wrong premise, stated with confidence, that would have produced a wrong headline had it not been checked at git. The pre-existing monitor watches THESIS-banner-date vs the trade docs' `Last Updated` lines; **this run supplies the missing consequence — the line is not cosmetic, it is what downstream readers and spawners use to decide whether to trust the doc.** Check it against `git log` for the file every run, not against its own text.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 15, upgraded at closeout) — INSIDE A FROZEN INTERIOR, A BANNER/INTERIOR *CONTRADICTION* IS STILL EDIT-WORTHY; MERE STALENESS IS NOT.** *(SAM ruling on Run-15 flag 4 — the reusable rule, not the instance.)* Once a doc is compressed-to-history, interior text that is simply **dated** is protected by the ruling and must not be flagged. But interior text asserting **in present voice** something the banner **explicitly denies** — at Run 15, STRATEGY L297's *"leg-1 tail — now 55,412 away"* / *"It RECLAIMED MED-HIGH"* sitting eight lines under a banner saying leg-1 FIRED — is a **correctness bug**, and it earns an edit. **Discriminator: present tense + direct denial of a banner assertion. Not age.** ⚠️ **Scope the strike narrowly** — SAM's first pass struck SAM-31 (yen-haven re-couple) along with the dead frame and caught it on re-read; SAM-31 is **still genuinely UNFIRED**. ⚠️ **And grade the defect honestly:** I surfaced this as an optional stylistic residual "SAM may reasonably decline." It was a contradiction, and under-grading it to appear deferential is its own failure mode — **flag it at its true class and let the ruling decide scope.**
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 15) — DELIVER BOTH WAYS: `SendMessage` TO THE COORDINATOR *AND* THE MEMORY FILE.** At Run 15 METSUKE wrote a complete report to `METSUKE_MEMORY.md` and went idle without messaging. Nothing was lost, but the coordinator had to go hunting for the deliverable. **The file half is the record; the message half is the delivery; neither substitutes for the other.** Per root CLAUDE.md's spawned-agent rule, delivery is the **final action before going idle** — never idle "holding" a finished report.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 15) — VERIFY DOC VINTAGE AT GIT BEFORE READING THE DOC, AND BEFORE TRUSTING THE SPAWN BRIEF.** *(This is the standing form of the flag-5 finding; the older `Last Updated` monitor above is the symptom, this is the procedure.)* **Step zero of every run:** `git log --format="%h %ad %s" --date=short -5 -- <the docs>`. Then compare against (a) the docs' own `Last Updated:` lines and (b) **what the spawn brief asserts about their vintage**. At Run 15 the stamps read 8/02 while the files carried 8/07 + 8/10 edits, **and the brief was built from those stamps** — it instructed METSUKE to headline that Run 14 had never been applied, which was false. **A header is a claim; git is the evidence; a brief that inherited the claim inherits the error.** A mismatch is itself the first flag of the run. *(Same shape one layer down: at Run-15 closeout SAM retracted "`ESTAT_APPID` absent ⇒ cannot pull" after checking one location and inferring a dead capability without running the script — **inferred-dead is not dead.**)*
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 15) — THE "DELETE, DON'T REFRESH" FIX IS THE ONE THAT ACTUALLY HOLDS, AND SIBLINGS DON'T INHERIT IT.** CALENDAR de-staled its Sep-18 BOJ row on 8/13 by **DELETING the embedded pricing figures** rather than refreshing them, explicitly because "that one number had already gone stale on four surfaces." TRADE's parallel cell was de-staled 8/10 by **refreshing** — and re-embedded `Sep cum 45.8%, Oct 76.7%, as-of 8/7`, which was stale again within 4 days and now sits under a STATUS ⛔ do-not-cite. **When a canonical surface fixes a recurring-stale figure by deletion, that is a fleet instruction, not a local edit — grep every sibling for the figure family and delete there too.** Recurring-figure classes (BOJ OIS %, CFTC %-of-peak) should live as pointers, never as inline restatements.

- **🆕 NEW (post Run 16) — A RULING GOVERNS THE NEXT WRITE, NOT THE EXISTING STATE. PAIR EVERY RULING WITH A RETROACTIVE SWEEP.** The 8/7 compress ruling changed what future runs may *flag* and changed **nothing already on the books**: ten PENDING sections carrying **97 items** stayed open for months, describing corrections to interiors the ruling had frozen. Nobody was wrong; nobody swept. **Whenever SAM issues a ruling that changes what counts as a defect — compress-to-history, VOID, RETIRED, do-not-cite — the same session must ask "what does this retire on the books?" and close it by adjudication, item by item, with artifact evidence.** ⚠️ **Close by adjudication, never by deletion-for-tidiness** — a closed item with a stated class and a grep behind it is an audit trail; a deleted one is a hole. And ⚠️ **a STILL-LIVE count of zero is not "nothing is live"** — say where the live exposure moved to, or the zero reads as an all-clear it never was.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 16) — THE BANNER'S NAMED ADJUDICATOR IS A DATED CLAIM WITH AN EXPIRY, AND IT EXPIRES SILENTLY.** The Run-15 fix dated the Meiji-floor certification and pointed a reader at *"the 20Y auction 8/20, then the 30Y on 9/3."* **That is a forward pointer sitting in current voice inside a frozen document — the instant 8/20 ran, the banner began telling readers to wait for a verdict that already existed.** ⚠️ And the failure is silent in a specific way: **a ⚪ NO-VERDICT looks identical to "not yet run"** at the banner, so the surface cannot distinguish *pending* from *asked-and-answered-nothing*. **Every run: take each adjudicator the banner names, check its date against today, and check its RESULT — a NO-VERDICT still discharges the pointer.** Same class as `[[finding_dated_carry_item_has_no_expiry_check]]`: a carried assertion is a string, and reading it never evaluates it.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 16) — CERTIFICATION ASYMMETRY: FIX ONE, LEAVE THREE, AND THE UNFIXED ONES LOOK STRONGER BY CONTRAST.** Run-15's E2 said a DO-NOT-TOUCH certification needs an expiry rather than a permanent tense. **That was answered for exactly ONE of the banner's three certifications** (Meiji floor — dated + UNDER LIVE TEST). The **EWJ/TLT/Japan-banks watchlists** and the **oil-in-yen Phase-1/Phase-2 mechanism** still read in permanent tense — and oil-in-yen's subject has since MOVED (Brent back through $90 to $94.58, July TB deficit widened to −¥634.5B ⇒ Phase-1 re-arming). ⚠️ **The mechanism is still valid, so this is NOT a wrong certification — it is an undated one, and the asymmetry now makes the two undated legs read as MORE settled than the dated one.** When a fix of this class is applied to one instance, apply it to the whole certification list or state why not.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 16) — "FORWARD-ONLY" TABLES ARE WHERE A BANNER CONTRADICTION DOES THE MOST DAMAGE.** Run-15 established that only a banner/interior **contradiction** earns an edit inside a frozen doc. Run 16 adds *where to hunt for one*: the tables whose own headers say **"forward-only"** (`TRADE.md` § Key Dates, `STRATEGY.md` § Key Check Dates). A stale row anywhere else reads as history; a stale row in a table labelled forward-only reads as **what is still live**, which is exactly the misread the banner exists to prevent. **Grep the forward-only tables against all four banner corrections every run.**

- **🆕 NEW (post Run 17) — A ROW-SCOPED CORRECTION DOES NOT TRAVEL ONE LINE UP EITHER. READ THE ROW ABOVE AND THE ROW BELOW BEFORE CLOSING.** Run 16 found a fix that *"landed on three neighbours and skipped this one"* (the row below). Run 17 found the **exact inverse in both docs**: the Run-16 fix landed on the ⚰️ Sep-18 row and skipped the identical retire-check assertion in the row **immediately above** it. **Two runs, two directions, same defect** ⇒ the sibling-instance failure is not directional and cannot be caught by fixing "the neighbour you noticed." **Procedure: when a flag names a row in a table, grep the asserted STRING across both docs before reporting the fix complete** — that is how flag 1's `55,412` was found surviving two forward-table siblings four months after its first correction.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 17) — A DATED CERTIFICATION STILL ROTS; DATING IT ONLY MAKES THE ROT LEGIBLE.** Run 15 ruled a certification needs a date, not a permanent tense; Run 16 dated all three banner legs. **Run 17 found one of those dated legs (oil-in-yen) stating a DIRECTION — "Phase-1 re-arming, Brent $94.58" — that reversed within 7 days.** ⚠️ **The discipline is symmetric and load-bearing in BOTH directions: the subject moving toward the mechanism CONFIRMS the certification (Run 16), and the subject moving away DE-ARMS without refuting it (Run 17). Neither licenses reframing the mechanism.** **Watch every dated banner leg for a directional STATE, not just an expiry** — a state is what a reader takes as current; a date only tells them how old it is.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 17) — "CITE LIVE, DO NOT CARRY" IS A CONSUMER-SIDE RULE AND HAS A PUBLISHER-SIDE BLIND SPOT.** The standing hint stopped METSUKE hand-carrying a BOJ percentage for the second run running — it works. **What it cannot see is the publisher: the same session that re-based ~73% → ~87.5% and declared its own staleness caveat BACKWARDS left `STATUS.md:193` telling every reader to "Cite ~73% and FALLING."** ⚠️ **Check the publisher's OWN surfaces whenever a repeatedly-stale figure re-bases** — dashboard row vs forward-catalyst row vs threshold table. **Fifth surface for this one number.** The consumer rule protects the report; only a surface sweep protects the file.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 18) — A "PROTECTED STALENESS" STAMP DOES NOT SURVIVE A CANONICAL RULING WRITTEN AFTER IT.** Run 17 stamped ~40-54% / ~60% BOJ figures in the two forward-only tables as "PROTECTED STALENESS … NOT re-flagged" — a self-consistent ruling at the time. **STATUS 9/1 then declared a fleet-wide rule: "no BOJ-pricing figure is restated in prose anywhere. … A cite-instruction cell is the LAST place a dead figure should survive."** The Run-17 stamp is now under a superseding rule; the figures inside it are still protected as audit trail, but the cell restatement is not. **Every run: check whether ANY canonical STATUS/THESIS ruling written between last run and this one supersedes a previously-applied stamp.** Same structural class as post-Run-16 "a ruling governs the next write, not the existing state" — applied to my OWN stamps.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 18) — A WRONG-FROM-THE-START FIGURE INSIDE A STAMP TRAVELS UNTIL THE PUBLISHER RE-EXAMINES.** The 8/27 stamp on the oil-in-yen banner leg carried $87.30 as the 8/27 Brent close; WALTER's 9/1 correction (`SIG-W-20260828-013`) found the 8/27 close was $89.70 BZV26, and my $87.30 matched NEITHER contract at any tick. **The figure travelled between two docs and one internal audit note ("THREE inconsistent oil states")** because the stamp was correct-at-the-time inside its own frame, and no downstream reader had a reason to re-verify. **When a canonical CORRECTION packet lands from a peer (WALTER, PROME, RED) that names a specific figure or contract, grep every doc's stamps for that figure — not just live cells.** Stamps have a longer half-life than live cells and are less-checked precisely because they read as "settled."
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 18) — A BANNER FIGURE ROT (Run-15's rule) IS TRIGGERED BY A SERIES-HIGH BREAK, NOT ONLY BY A DIRECT SUPERSESSION.** Run 15 established: *"every figure in the banner against the current basis; every 'still valid' certification against what has happened since it was written."* Run 18 adds the trigger form: **a series-high figure quoted in the banner rots the moment a NEW series high prints, even if the certification is unchanged.** The banner's *"MOF-basis series high is CONFIRMED at 4.096 [8/18]"* was correct until 9/1 04:00 GMT, wrong at 9/1 close. **On every run, grep the banner for any figure adjacent to "series high" / "clearing the prior" / "highest since" / "record" and verify at STATUS's current basis** — these are the tell-tale phrases of figures that rot on a single print rather than a re-derivation.
- **🆕 NEW (post Run 17) — AN UNMARKED "DONE" BLOCK IS INVISIBLE TO THE ROLL TOOL, SO A DISPOSITIONED BACKLOG STILL COUNTS AS LIVE.** All three Run-16 flags were applied (verified at the artifacts), the disposition sits at the head of this file — and `## PENDING from Run 16` carries no CLOSED header, so `subagent_memory_roll.py` reports **"nothing terminal to roll"** on a **251K** file. **Rule 4 is working as designed (silence is never closure); the gap is that the DISPOSITION and the SECTION live in different places.** ⚠️ **At every closeout, check whether the previous run's PENDING section has been marked — flag it if not.** This is the Run-16 E1 ruling-sweep defect reproduced inside METSUKE's own state file.
